## S1-S3 lessons the executor must honor

1. **CLI scripts**: invoke with `--help` to confirm parseable.
2. **SQL fragments in Python strings**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` or sanity-check types (no `max(uuid)` etc.).
3. **asyncpg pool against `DATABASE_URL`**: pass `statement_cache_size=0` (Supabase port 6543 is transaction-mode).
4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs** — use `hashlib.sha1`.
5. **Schema filters in validation queries** use `'mediator'` not `'public'`.
6. **For psql migrations against Supabase**: use port 5432 session-mode for DDL.
7. **Don't introduce scope creep**: no unrelated features or unused fields in shared classes.
8. **NO defensive fallbacks**: per S3 lesson, don't add `topic_id IS NULL` or similar permissive filters.
9. **For verify-human-heavy plans**: keep criterion count manageable (~10-15); each criterion should be cheaply auto-verifiable from artifacts.
10. **S3 cutover is committed**: every artifact read goes through `topic_filter.join_artifact_topics()`. New code follows this pattern, don't reintroduce direct reads.
11. **Pytest baseline**: 534 pass / 0 fail / 3 skip on s3-join-cutover HEAD. S4 must maintain or improve.

- Any test that was passing on `s3-join-cutover` HEAD (commit 52d6dab) starts failing — STOP, report.
- `consult_perspective` enumeration test fails to enumerate any field — STOP, fix (this is a real correctness gap).
- Tool-schema changes break mediator behavior in eval (if eval suite available) — STOP, audit.
- Solo bot infra accidentally touches prod (any non-staging DB write) — STOP, revert.
- Negative-permission test (career-scoped bot trying to read relationship artifacts) PASSES (it should FAIL — that's the point) — STOP, fix authorization layer.

# Implementation Plan: Sprint 4 — Authorization + topic status + solo bot pre-flight

## Overview

S4 lands the authorization plumbing (`ReadScopes`/`WriteScopes`), the `topic_status` write/read surface, a leak-safe `consult_perspective` clone, and the staging-only pre-flight for a second ("coach") solo bot. With one production topic (`relationship`), `"own"` and `"all"` resolve identically — so the goal is to plumb the contracts and emit nothing observable, while a no-op second `BotSpec` with `read_scopes.topics={"career"}` proves the layer rejects cross-topic reads.

Current state of repo (s3-join-cutover tip 52d6dab):
- `TurnContext` (`app/services/turn_context.py:17`) already carries `read_scopes`/`write_scopes`/`cross_topic_policy`/`dyad_id` as `Any | None` placeholders. S4 puts real dataclasses behind them.
- `BotSpec`/`ReadScopes`/`WriteScopes` in `app/bots/base.py:18-49` exist; `ReadScopes` has only `allow_cross_topic_status_injection`, `WriteScopes` is empty. S4 fleshes them out per §6.
- `consult_perspective.py:105-132` reconstructs `TurnContext` field-by-field — leak risk; the locked plan replaces it with `dataclasses.replace(ctx, …)`.
- Topic-scoped artifact reads already go through `topic_filter.join_artifact_topics(...)` and bind `ctx.primary_topic_id` on the last parameter. S4 must follow that pattern when emitting cross-topic peek/status SQL.
- `user_bot_state.paused` table already exists (`migrations/0022_topic_status_user_bot_state.sql:34`) and is read by `system_state.user_bot_paused`; the WRITE path is the deferred S2b item to ship here.
- `topic_status` table already exists (`migrations/0022_topic_status_user_bot_state.sql:10`), but no tool / write path / hot-context block / SQL helpers exist yet — all greenfield in S4.
- The 51-entry `TOOL_DISPATCH` lives in `app/services/tools/registry.py:103`; the §16.5-claimed "~19 read-phase tools" is `READ_PHASE_TOOLS` at line 157 (count = 18). Two additional read surfaces hide in `write_tools.py`: `list_scheduled_tasks` (line 1456) and the read parts of bridge tools. Per locked decision #7, `scope` only goes on artifact-reading tools; `list_scheduled_tasks`, `recent_activity`, `search_messages`, `search_emojis`, `get_bot_actions`, `send_message_part`, `check_oob`, `summarize_oob_topics`, `consult_perspective` are scope-irrelevant and skipped.
- No staging supabase project is configured (`supabase/` is empty besides `.temp/`); see assumptions on the staging track.

Constraints (locked, do not relitigate): peek window default 14d, status cap N=5, `set_topic_status` folded into `record`, solo #2 = "coach" / `career` / Discord, solo crisis → refer dyadic + external, `scope` undocumented this sprint, staging-only solo provisioning, no multi-topic writes.

## Phase 1: Authorization scaffolding (types, ctx wiring, lint)

### Step 1: Audit current authorization stubs (`app/bots/base.py`, `app/services/turn_context.py`, `app/services/tools/registry.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** `ReadScopes`/`WriteScopes` (`app/bots/base.py:18`) and `TurnContext.read_scopes/write_scopes` (`app/services/turn_context.py:31`). Confirm they are `Any | None` today.
2. **Catalogue** the 18 entries in `READ_PHASE_TOOLS` (`app/services/tools/registry.py:157`) plus `list_scheduled_tasks` (in `write_tools.py` but used in `READ_PHASE_TOOLS`). Mark which take artifact reads (`get_memories`, `get_observations`, `get_distillations`, `get_oob`, `summarize_oob_topics`, `list_themes`, `get_theme`, `list_watch_items`, `list_bridge_candidates`, `get_self_model`) versus scope-irrelevant (`search_messages`, `search_emojis`, `recent_activity`, `get_bot_actions`, `send_message_part`, `check_oob`, `consult_perspective`, `list_scheduled_tasks`).
3. **Confirm** that every artifact read in `read_tools.py` already binds `ctx.primary_topic_id` on the last param (we saw this in lines 533, 548, 554, 558, 595, 623, 655, 707, 755, 773) — no SQL changes are needed for the `"own"` scope; the work is gating logic above the SQL.

### Step 2: Define real `ReadScopes` / `WriteScopes` (`app/bots/base.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Replace** the placeholder dataclasses with frozen-dataclass shapes that match §6:
   ```python
   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class ReadScopes:
       topics: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"own"})  # "own" | "all" | explicit slugs
       allow_cross_topic_peek: bool = False
       allow_cross_topic_status_injection: bool = False

   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class WriteScopes:
       topics: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"own"})
       require_reason_for_cross_topic: bool = False
   ```
2. **Add** `cross_topic_policy: str | None = None` (values: `None | "peek" | "write"`) to `BotSpec`. Keep `bot_spec_version` bump small (e.g. `"1.1.0"`).
3. **Update** `app/bots/registry.py` `populate_mediator_spec_from_db` (line 70-71) to pass the mediator's real scopes (Step 3 below).

### Step 3: Mediator BotSpec gets real scopes (`app/bots/mediator.py`, `app/bots/registry.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Set** the mediator instantiation (`app/bots/mediator.py:99`) to:
   ```python
   MEDIATOR_BOT = MediatorBotSpec(
       bot_id="mediator",
       prompt_renderer=render_system_prompt,
       step_instructions=MEDIATOR_STEP_INSTRUCTIONS,
       cross_topic_policy="peek",
       read_scopes=ReadScopes(
           topics=frozenset({"own"}),
           allow_cross_topic_peek=True,
           allow_cross_topic_status_injection=True,
       ),
       write_scopes=WriteScopes(
           topics=frozenset({"relationship"}),
           require_reason_for_cross_topic=True,
       ),
   )
   ```
2. **Mirror** the same values in `populate_mediator_spec_from_db` (`app/bots/registry.py:70-71`).

### Step 4: Plumb scopes onto every TurnContext build site (`app/services/agentic.py`, `app/services/recovery.py`, `app/services/scheduled_jobs.py`, `app/services/checkins.py`, `app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Find** every `TurnContext(...)` constructor (use `grep -n "TurnContext(" app/`). For each, copy `read_scopes`/`write_scopes`/`cross_topic_policy` from the resolved `BotSpec` (most call sites already pass `bot_spec=spec`).
2. **Verify** ctx-build helpers in `recovery.py`, `scheduled_jobs.py`, `checkins.py`, and `inbound.py` propagate the new fields (defaulting to mediator's spec when no other bot is wired).

### Step 5: Authorization gate at the artifact read SQL boundary (`app/services/tools/read_tools.py`, `app/services/tools/write_tools.py`, `app/services/tools/registry.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** a `scope` Pydantic field to artifact-reading tool inputs in `tool_schemas.py` (the 10 inputs from Step 1.2). Type: `Literal["own", "all"] = "own"`. **Do not** add anything to the tool description string (locked decision #6 — undocumented this sprint).
2. **At the function entry** of each artifact-reading tool (e.g. `get_memories`, `list_themes`, etc. in `read_tools.py`), enforce:
   - If `args.scope == "all"` and `"all" not in ctx.read_scopes.topics`, return `is_error=True, error="scope_denied: bot lacks 'all' read scope"`.
   - If `args.scope` matches an explicit slug not in `ctx.read_scopes.topics`, same error.
   - With one topic and `"own"`, behavior is unchanged. (The §16.5 phrasing "verify plumbing without changing output" — that's the goal.)
3. **For every write tool in `write_tools.py`**: at function entry, assert `ctx.primary_topic_slug` ∈ `ctx.write_scopes.topics` (or `"own"` shorthand resolves). On miss → raise `ToolCallRejected(... "write_scope_denied ...")`. Mediator with `write_scopes.topics={"relationship"}` passes; the negative-permission test (Step 13) trips this.
4. **Centralize** the gate as a small helper `app/services/tools/scope_guard.py` so the per-tool change is one line.

### Step 6: Lint — every DB-touching tool fn accepts and uses `ctx` (`tests/test_s4_scope_lint.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a new test that walks `TOOL_DISPATCH` (excluding `update_turn_plan`), inspects each callable's signature, and asserts the first positional arg is named `ctx` and the body (via `inspect.getsource`) references `ctx.` at least once. This catches a regression where someone adds a tool that ignores the context.

## Phase 2: `consult_perspective` clone + enumeration test

### Step 7: Add a `replace_ctx` clone helper (`app/services/turn_context.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** at the bottom of `turn_context.py`:
   ```python
   from dataclasses import replace as _dc_replace
   def replace_ctx(ctx: TurnContext, **overrides) -> TurnContext:
       return _dc_replace(ctx, **overrides)
   ```
   The helper is the *only* sanctioned way to derive a new `TurnContext` from an existing one. (`dataclasses.replace` is also a fine direct call site; the test in Step 9 lints the field-by-field constructor specifically.)

### Step 8: Rewrite `consult_perspective` to clone (`app/services/tools/consult_perspective.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Replace** lines 105-132 with:
   ```python
   from app.services.turn_context import replace_ctx
   ...
   consult_ctx = replace_ctx(
       ctx,
       current_step="consult",
       incremental_sending_enabled=False,
       send_typing_indicator=False,
       before_paced_send=None,
       sent_message_parts=[],
       triggering_message_ids=list(ctx.triggering_message_ids),
       protected_owner_ids=list(ctx.protected_owner_ids or []),
       trigger_metadata={**dict(ctx.trigger_metadata), "_inside_consult": True},
   )
   ```
2. **Confirm** nothing else in the file constructs `TurnContext` directly.

### Step 9: Enumeration test — no field is dropped on clone (`tests/test_consult_perspective_clone.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Write** a test that, given a fully-populated `TurnContext`, calls the same clone path used by `consult_perspective` (factor it into a small private helper if needed), then iterates **every** field in `dataclasses.fields(TurnContext)` and asserts the value on the cloned context matches the source (with the four overridden fields treated as expected-different).
2. **Add a second lint test** that scans `app/` for `TurnContext(` constructor calls outside `app/services/turn_context.py` and fails if any are found (with an allowlist for the canonical builder in `agentic.py` and any other legitimate top-level constructor, which we'll keep at one).
3. This lint is the §16.5 stop condition: a regression that re-introduces a field-by-field clone fails CI immediately.

## Phase 3: `topic_status` write/read surface

### Step 10: Schema (`set_topic_status` tool I/O) (`tool_schemas.py`, `app/services/tools/registry.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** to `tool_schemas.py`:
   ```python
   class SetTopicStatusInput(BaseModel):
       scope: Literal["user", "dyad"]
       user_id: UUID | None = None  # required if scope=="user"
       headline: str = Field(..., max_length=80)
       body: str = Field("", max_length=300)
       # (topic is implicit: ctx.primary_topic_id)

   class SetTopicStatusOutput(BaseModel):
       is_error: bool = False
       error: str | None = None
       status_id: UUID | None = None
       headline: str | None = None
       body: str | None = None
       updated_at: str | None = None  # ISO
   ```
2. **Register** in `TOOL_REGISTRY`, `TOOL_DISPATCH`, `WRITE_PHASE_TOOLS`, and `RECORD_WRITE_TOOLS` (so it lands in the `record` step). NOT in `SCHEDULE_TOOLS`.
3. **Description** (per-bot gating, see Step 12): "Update or replace the current relationship status for this topic. Headline ≤ 80 chars, body ≤ 300 chars. Use at most once per turn during the `record` step when status has materially changed."

### Step 11: Write impl (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Implement** `set_topic_status(ctx, args)` using an `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (topic_id, user_id) DO UPDATE` (when `scope=="user"`) or `ON CONFLICT (topic_id, dyad_id) DO UPDATE` (when `scope=="dyad"`). The partial unique indexes already exist (`migrations/0022_topic_status_user_bot_state.sql:24-30`).
2. **Enforce** the XOR by validating `(args.scope == "user") == (args.user_id is not None) and (args.scope == "dyad") == (ctx.dyad_id is not None)`.
3. **Audit** length: `headline ≤ 80`, `body ≤ 300` (also Pydantic-enforced); reject early with `ToolCallRejected("validation_too_long")`.
4. **Re-fetch** the row to populate the output (so callers see `updated_at`).
5. **Log** via the existing `_log_tool_call` helper for parity with other writers.

### Step 12: Mediator-only registration via per-bot allowlist (`app/services/tools/registry.py`, `app/bots/base.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a `BotSpec.tool_allowlist: frozenset[str] | None = None` field — `None` means "all step-allowed tools". On the mediator, default to `None` (everything stays available).
2. **In** `_step_allowed(ctx)` (`registry.py:270`), if `ctx.bot_spec.tool_allowlist is not None`, intersect the result with it. The coach bot in Step 16 will use this to *omit* `set_topic_status`.
3. **Fold** the tool into `MEDIATOR_RECORD_INSTRUCTION` (`app/bots/mediator.py:74`) — append a sentence: "If the relationship's current state has materially shifted this turn, call `set_topic_status` exactly once with a short headline and optional body." (Locked decision B.)

### Step 13: Tests (`tests/test_set_topic_status.py`, `tests/test_scope_negative.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Test** `set_topic_status` happy paths for user and dyad scopes; ON CONFLICT upserts; rejection on body > 300 / headline > 80.
2. **Negative-permission test** (the mid-sprint checkpoint): construct a `BotSpec` for the imagined coach (`read_scopes.topics=frozenset({"career"})`, `write_scopes.topics=frozenset({"career"})`), wire it onto a `TurnContext`, and assert that calling `get_memories`/`list_themes`/`get_oob`/etc. with `ctx.primary_topic_slug="relationship"` returns `is_error=True, error="scope_denied: ..."`. Stop condition: if this test *passes* (i.e., the call returns artifacts), the authorization layer is broken.

## Phase 4: Hot context — status block + cross-topic peek code paths

### Step 14: Status block render (own topic) (`app/services/hot_context.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a `topic_status` field to `HotContext` (`app/services/hot_context.py:24`): `topic_status: dict[str, Any] | None = None`.
2. **Fetch** the row for `(ctx.primary_topic_id, ctx.dyad_id)` when dyad-scoped, OR `(ctx.primary_topic_id, ctx.user.id)` when participants are solo; in build, prefer dyad-row first (it's the §7 default for the mediator), fall back to user-row.
3. **Render** in the existing `render_hot_context` function under a `## Topic status` section showing `headline` and (if non-empty) `body` and `last_updated_at`. With no row yet → emit nothing.

### Step 15: Cross-topic status injection + peek code paths (`app/services/hot_context.py`, `app/services/cross_thread_privacy.py` or new helper)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** a helper `fetch_cross_topic_status(pool, *, dyad_id, user_id, exclude_topic_id, cap=5)`: returns up to N=5 most-recently-active `topic_status` rows for the user's other topics. Code path is live; with one topic in prod, it returns `[]`.
2. **Gate** the call: only run when `ctx.bot_spec.read_scopes.allow_cross_topic_status_injection` is True. Render under `## Other topics (status)` block; emits nothing if list empty.
3. **Add** a `peek_other_topics(pool, *, since=now-14d, dyad_id, user_id, exclude_topic_id, cap=5)`: returns a thin summary (topic slug + most recent message timestamp + last `topic_status.headline`). 14d default is locked.
4. **Gate** with `ctx.bot_spec.read_scopes.allow_cross_topic_peek` and `ctx.bot_spec.cross_topic_policy == "peek"`. Render under `## Peek`. With one topic → empty list → render nothing.
5. **Tests** in `tests/test_hot_context_cross_topic.py`: with a stubbed pool returning rows, ensure the blocks render correctly; with empty results, ensure no header appears (no leaked empty sections).

## Phase 5: Per-(user, bot) pause WRITE path

### Step 16: Write helpers + admin route + tool (`app/services/system_state.py`, `app/routers/admin.py`, `app/services/tools/write_tools.py`, `tool_schemas.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** to `system_state.py`:
   ```python
   async def set_user_bot_paused(pool, user_id: UUID, bot_id: str, paused: bool) -> None:
       await pool.execute(
           """INSERT INTO user_bot_state (user_id, bot_id, paused, updated_at)
              VALUES ($1, $2, $3, now())
              ON CONFLICT (user_id, bot_id) DO UPDATE
              SET paused = EXCLUDED.paused, updated_at = now()""",
           user_id, bot_id, paused,
       )
   ```
2. **Admin endpoint** in `app/routers/admin.py`: `POST /admin/user-bot-pause` accepting `user_id`, `bot_id`, `paused` (form fields, behind existing `authenticate_admin`).
3. **Optional tool** `pause_user_bot` (locked decision: "off by default") — register only on the mediator's allowlist, restricted to admin-tagged paths. **Skip** the tool if we agree pause is operator-only and the admin endpoint is sufficient; defer to ASK question #1.
4. **Tests** in `tests/test_user_bot_pause_write.py` to round-trip set/clear via the helper and admin endpoint.

## Phase 6: Solo "coach" bot — staging-only pre-flight

### Step 17: Staging seed migration (`migrations/0031_coach_staging_seed.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Write** a migration that, when applied **only to staging**, inserts:
   - `channels` row for the new Discord transport (placeholder channel id).
   - `bots` row `id='coach', display_name='Coach'`.
   - `topics` row `slug='career', display_name='Career'`.
   - `bot_bindings` linking coach ↔ career on the new channel.
2. **Guard** with a `DO $$ BEGIN IF current_database() NOT LIKE '%staging%' THEN RETURN; END IF; … END $$;` block OR commit-only-to-staging-folder convention. Locked decision #8: must not promote to prod. (See assumption #4.)
3. **Document** in the migration header: applies only to staging, S5 owns the prod promotion.

### Step 18: Coach BotSpec (no prompt, no hot context yet) (`app/bots/coach.py`, `app/bots/registry.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a minimal `coach.py` with a `BotSpec` whose `read_scopes=ReadScopes(topics=frozenset({"career"}))`, `write_scopes=WriteScopes(topics=frozenset({"career"}))`, `cross_topic_policy=None`, `tool_allowlist=frozenset(... )` (omit `set_topic_status` if locked decision is "mediator-only initially"; otherwise include).
2. **Register** in `BOT_SPECS` only when `staging` env flag is set (env var `STAGING=1` or similar — already detectable via `app/staging.py`).
3. **No prompt renderer** — supply a stub that raises `NotImplementedError("coach prompt lands in S5")`. Step 19 doesn't invoke runtime turns; the spec is only used by tests and the OOB-guardrail check.

### Step 19: Transport scaffolding test (`tests/test_coach_transport_staging.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Write** a test that: builds a TurnContext with `bot_spec=COACH_BOT`, `primary_topic_slug="career"`, then calls the OOB guardrail (`app/services/oob_check.py:check_oob_with_policy`) with a known-bad message. Assert OOB fires. This satisfies the staging-only OOB guardrail check at code level without requiring a real Discord round-trip.
2. **Mark** as `pytest.mark.staging` or skip if `STAGING` env not set. Locked decision: "do not block on staging access".

### Step 20: Observability sanity (`scripts/check_per_bot_panels.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** a tiny CLI script that queries `turn_events` / `tool_calls` (or wherever the S2a panes pull from) grouped by `bot_id`, prints counts for `mediator` and `coach`, and exits non-zero if the `bot_id` column is missing or NULL on rows tagged with the coach bot. Per locked plan: "code-level check is sufficient; do not require actual Grafana access".

## Phase 7: Bring-up + branch hygiene

### Step 21: Create the working branch
**Scope:** Small
1. **First action** of execution: `git checkout -b s4-auth-status-preflight` off `52d6dab` (current HEAD of s3-join-cutover). Per orchestration block.

### Step 22: Out-of-scope files stay untouched
**Scope:** Small
1. **Do not modify or `git add`**: `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_time_context.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tool_schemas.py` (the dirty version), `migrations/0019_feedback_reaction_context.sql`. (Note: `tool_schemas.py` itself is **in scope** — the "dirty" line in the orchestration block refers to the *uncommitted-on-disk* state. Confirm with Question #2 below.)

### Step 23: Run targeted tests early, then full suite
**Scope:** Small
1. **First**: `pytest tests/test_consult_perspective_clone.py tests/test_set_topic_status.py tests/test_scope_negative.py tests/test_s4_scope_lint.py -x` — these are the new sentinels.
2. **Then**: `pytest -x` against the whole suite. Baseline = 534 pass / 0 fail / 3 skip on `s3-join-cutover` HEAD. S4 must maintain or improve (likely lands at ~545+).

## Execution Order
1. **Phase 1** (Steps 1-6): authorization scaffolding. Cheapest, lowest-risk. Land this before anything else so the negative-permission test in Phase 3 can be authored against real types.
2. **Phase 2** (Steps 7-9): consult clone. Independent and small; can interleave with Phase 1.
3. **Phase 3** (Steps 10-13): `topic_status` + mediator allowlist + negative-permission test. Depends on Phase 1's `ReadScopes`/`WriteScopes` being real, and on Step 12's allowlist field.
4. **Phase 4** (Steps 14-15): hot-context rendering. Depends on `topic_status` write existing for the render-with-no-row case to be meaningful.
5. **Phase 5** (Step 16): per-(user, bot) pause WRITE path. Independent; can land any time after Phase 1.
6. **Phase 6** (Steps 17-20): staging pre-flight. Independent; can land any time, but ship after Phase 1-3 so the coach BotSpec uses real authorization types.
7. **Phase 7** (Steps 21-23): branch & test hygiene; Step 21 actually runs *first*, but listed last so it's not confused with implementation phases.

## Validation Order
1. New per-tool unit tests (`test_consult_perspective_clone`, `test_set_topic_status`, `test_scope_negative`, `test_s4_scope_lint`, `test_hot_context_cross_topic`, `test_user_bot_pause_write`).
2. Full pytest suite — must match or beat 534/0/3 baseline.
3. `python -m app.staging --help` parses (S1-S3 lesson #1).
4. Grep for `TurnContext(` outside the canonical builder — must return one allow-listed location (Step 9's lint test).
5. Tool-schema regression: dump `to_anthropic_tools(READ_PHASE_TOOLS)` before/after; diff should show only `scope` parameters added on the 10 artifact-reading tools, all with empty description suffixes (locked decision #6).
6. Mediator eval run if available — confirm no behavior regression from the added `scope` parameter (per Risk section in §16.5).

PROD promotion of the solo bot (S5).\n\n## S1-S3 lessons the executor must honor\n\n1. **CLI scripts**: invoke with `--help` to confirm parseable.\n2. **SQL fragments in Python strings**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` or sanity-check types (no `max(uuid)` etc.).\n3. **asyncpg pool against `DATABASE_URL`**: pass `statement_cache_size=0` (Supabase port 6543 is transaction-mode).\n4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs** \u2014 use `hashlib.sha1`.\n5. **Schema filters in validation queries** use `'mediator'` not `'public'`.\n6. **For psql migrations against Supabase**: use port 5432 session-mode for DDL.\n7. **Don't introduce scope creep**: no unrelated features or unused fields in shared classes.\n8. **NO defensive fallbacks**: per S3 lesson, don't add `topic_id IS NULL` or similar permissive filters.\n9. **For verify-human-heavy plans**: keep criterion count manageable (~10-15); each criterion should be cheaply auto-verifiable from artifacts.\n10. **S3 cutover is committed**: every artifact read goes through `topic_filter.join_artifact_topics()`. New code follows this pattern, don't reintroduce direct reads.\n11. **Pytest baseline**: 534 pass / 0 fail / 3 skip on s3-join-cutover HEAD. S4 must maintain or improve.\n\n## Stop conditions\n\n- Any test that was passing on `s3-join-cutover` HEAD (commit 52d6dab) starts failing \u2014 STOP, report.\n- `consult_perspective` enumeration test fails to enumerate any field \u2014 STOP, fix (this is a real correctness gap).\n- Tool-schema changes break mediator behavior in eval (if eval suite available) \u2014 STOP, audit.\n- Solo bot infra accidentally touches prod (any non-staging DB write) \u2014 STOP, revert.\n- Negative-permission test (career-scoped bot trying to read relationship artifacts) PASSES (it should FAIL \u2014 that's the point) \u2014 STOP, fix authorization layer.",

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-019",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-026",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-031",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-034",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 4.5's `_row_matches_topic` helper falls back to global behavior when (table, id) is not in self.artifact_topics \u2014 explicitly for backward compatibility. this is sensible but creates a subtle hazard: a test that forgets to call link_topic for a new artifact sees global decay behavior with no warning, which could mask the very scoping bug the new test in step 12 is meant to catch. the decay scope test does call link_topic for both topics, so it's safe; future authors may not. worth recording but not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-3-join-cutover"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-036",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: flag-017 (decay handler backward-compat fallback) is downgraded to accepted maintainability debt with an inline warning comment per step 4.5. the warning text explicitly tells future authors to call link_topic. acceptable, but a noisy assertion (e.g. log a warning when topic_id present in sql but row has no link) would catch silent regressions more loudly. not blocking.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-3-join-cutover"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [

      "id": "DEBT-001",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",

    "subsystem": "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements",
    "total_occurrences": 9,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-001",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },

        "id": "DEBT-004",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the 0020 migration plan for user_identities omits the verified_at timestamptz column present in architecture doc \u00a712.2 ddl, while claiming 'exactly per \u00a73/\u00a712 ddl'. since account-linking workflows are deferred beyond s1, the column is not functionally load-bearing yet, but the claim of exact ddl match is incorrect.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-009",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the resolve_binding sql in app/services/routing.py uses limit 1 without order by. if a user ever has both a solo binding and a dyad binding to the same bot, the returned row is non-deterministic. the schema has no unique constraint preventing multiple bindings per (bot_id, user_id). since s1 only seeds one dyad binding and the module is not wired into inbound yet, this is latent future risk rather than an immediate s1 blocker.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 7,
    "plan_count": 4,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-002",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-011",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-019",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-026",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-031",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2b-constraints"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-034",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 4.5's `_row_matches_topic` helper falls back to global behavior when (table, id) is not in self.artifact_topics \u2014 explicitly for backward compatibility. this is sensible but creates a subtle hazard: a test that forgets to call link_topic for a new artifact sees global decay behavior with no warning, which could mask the very scoping bug the new test in step 12 is meant to catch. the decay scope test does call link_topic for both topics, so it's safe; future authors may not. worth recording but not a blocker.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-3-join-cutover"
        ]

        ]

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

## S1-S3 lessons the executor must honor

1. **CLI scripts**: invoke with `--help` to confirm parseable.
2. **SQL fragments in Python strings**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` or sanity-check types (no `max(uuid)` etc.).
3. **asyncpg pool against `DATABASE_URL`**: pass `statement_cache_size=0` (Supabase port 6543 is transaction-mode).
4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs** — use `hashlib.sha1`.
5. **Schema filters in validation queries** use `'mediator'` not `'public'`.
6. **For psql migrations against Supabase**: use port 5432 session-mode for DDL.
7. **Don't introduce scope creep**: no unrelated features or unused fields in shared classes.
8. **NO defensive fallbacks**: per S3 lesson, don't add `topic_id IS NULL` or similar permissive filters.
9. **For verify-human-heavy plans**: keep criterion count manageable (~10-15); each criterion should be cheaply auto-verifiable from artifacts.
10. **S3 cutover is committed**: every artifact read goes through `topic_filter.join_artifact_topics()`. New code follows this pattern, don't reintroduce direct reads.
11. **Pytest baseline**: 534 pass / 0 fail / 3 skip on s3-join-cutover HEAD. S4 must maintain or improve.

- Solo bot infra accidentally touches prod (any non-staging DB write) — STOP, revert.
- Negative-permission test (career-scoped bot trying to read relationship artifacts) PASSES (it should FAIL — that's the point) — STOP, fix authorization layer.

# Implementation Plan: Sprint 4 — Authorization + topic status + solo bot pre-flight (rev 2)

## Overview

S4 lands the authorization plumbing (`ReadScopes`/`WriteScopes`), the `topic_status` write/read surface, a leak-safe `consult_perspective` clone, and the staging-only pre-flight for a second ("coach") solo bot. With one production topic (`relationship`), the mediator's `topics={"own"}` always resolves to its primary slug, so behavior is unchanged in prod; the negative-permission test uses a contrived coach BotSpec (`topics={"career"}` against `primary_topic_slug="relationship"`) to prove the gate denies.

**Critical correctness fixes baked into this revision (from gate iter 1):**
- The read-scope gate **resolves `scope="own"` against `ctx.primary_topic_slug` and checks membership in `read_scopes.topics`** (formerly silently bypassed the default path). Specifically: `"own" ∈ topics` (mediator-style) OR `ctx.primary_topic_slug ∈ topics` (explicit slug) OR `"all" ∈ topics` ⇒ allow; else deny. This is the rule that makes the negative-permission stop condition fire.
- The write-scope gate applies **only to artifact writers** (memory/observation/distillation/theme/watch_item/oob/bridge_candidate/topic_status). Transport, scheduling, and `log_feedback` are explicitly out of scope (they don't touch the artifact tables that `read_scopes`/`write_scopes` govern).
- The gate helper is **None-permissive**: if `ctx.read_scopes is None` or `ctx.write_scopes is None`, the gate no-ops. This preserves the ~15 legacy test fixtures (e.g. `test_send_outbound.py:537,626,677,732`, `test_s1_code_shape.py`, `test_turn_plan.py`, `test_evals_capture.py`, `tests/_scope_helpers.py`) without a fixture sweep. Production builders (`agentic.py`, `recovery.py`, `scheduled_jobs.py`, `checkins.py`, `inbound.py`) always populate scopes from the BotSpec — a new lint test enforces that.
- `set_topic_status` upsert includes `last_updated_by_bot_id = ctx.bot_id` (NOT NULL per `migrations/0022:18`).
- `set_topic_status` is added only to `WRITE_PHASE_TOOLS`; `RECORD_WRITE_TOOLS` derives automatically (`registry.py:223`).
- Staging seed migration places **all DML inside the DO block** (RETURN exits only PL/pgSQL); no statements outside the guard.

**Resolved metadata questions (locked in this revision):**
- **Q1 (tool_schemas.py dirty file):** The orchestration block's "do not touch" list refers to the *uncommitted-on-disk* diff. First step of execution: `git stash push -u tool_schemas.py tests/test_hot_context.py tests/test_time_context.py tests/test_tools.py migrations/0019_feedback_reaction_context.sql` to set them aside. `tool_schemas.py` is then in-scope on a clean base for adding `SetTopicStatusInput`/`Output` and `scope` fields. Stash is restored to the working tree (not committed) after the sprint.
- **Q4 (staging migration convention):** Numbered file `migrations/0031_coach_staging_seed.sql` with an `IF current_database() LIKE '%staging%'` guard wrapping **all DML inside a single `DO $$ … END $$` block**. No staging subfolder is added — the existing flat `migrations/` layout is preserved.

**Acknowledged trade-off (issue_hints-1):** Adding `scope: Literal["own","all"]` to artifact-reading Pydantic input models exposes the parameter name/type via `model_json_schema()` → Anthropic `input_schema`. This matches the literal lock decision #6 ("undocumented in tool descriptions"). The model sees the field exists; only the *description* is silent. Stated explicitly so reviewers don't mistake this for a leak.

Current repo state (s3-join-cutover tip 52d6dab):
- `TurnContext` (`app/services/turn_context.py:17`) already carries `read_scopes`/`write_scopes`/`cross_topic_policy`/`dyad_id` as `Any | None` placeholders.
- `BotSpec`/`ReadScopes`/`WriteScopes` in `app/bots/base.py:18-49` exist as stubs (ReadScopes only has `allow_cross_topic_status_injection`).
- `consult_perspective.py:105-132` reconstructs `TurnContext` field-by-field.
- `topic_status` table & partial unique indexes exist (`migrations/0022_topic_status_user_bot_state.sql:10-30`) but no write path yet.
- `user_bot_state.paused` table & read path exist (`system_state.user_bot_paused` at `system_state.py:35`); WRITE path is the S2b carryover.
- `READ_PHASE_TOOLS` has 18 entries (`registry.py:157`); 10 are artifact readers (`get_memories`, `get_observations`, `get_distillations`, `get_oob`, `summarize_oob_topics`, `list_themes`, `get_theme`, `list_watch_items`, `list_bridge_candidates`, `get_self_model`). The other 8 are scope-irrelevant.
- `WRITE_PHASE_TOOLS` has 31 entries (`registry.py:178-211`); per FLAG-S4-002, only **artifact writers** get the write gate: `add_memory`, `update_memory`, `supersede_memory`, `create_theme`, `update_theme`, `add_watch_item`, `update_watch_item`, `address_watch_item`, `log_observation`, `update_observation`, `add_distillation`, `update_distillation`, `revise_distillation`, `add_oob`, `update_oob`, `lift_oob`, `create_bridge_candidate`, `update_bridge_candidate`, `send_bridge_candidate`, `set_topic_status` (new). `update_user_style_notes` and `update_cross_thread_sharing_default` also write user-scoped state — include them. Excluded from the gate: `send_message_part`, `react_to_message`, `edit_outbound_message`, `delete_outbound_message`, `escalate_to_partner`, `log_feedback`, `explain_media_item`, `list_scheduled_tasks`, `schedule_checkin`, `cancel_scheduled_checkin`, `schedule_task`, `update_scheduled_task`, `cancel_scheduled_task`.

## Phase 1: Authorization scaffolding (types, ctx wiring, gate helper)

### Step 1: Audit + catalogue (`app/bots/base.py`, `app/services/turn_context.py`, `app/services/tools/registry.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** `ReadScopes`/`WriteScopes` (`app/bots/base.py:18`) and `TurnContext.read_scopes/write_scopes` (`app/services/turn_context.py:31`).
2. **Catalogue** artifact readers (10) and artifact writers (~20, list above) into two module-level frozensets in a new file `app/services/tools/scope_guard.py`.
3. **Confirm** that artifact reads bind `ctx.primary_topic_id` last (existing pattern at `read_tools.py:533, 548, 554, 558, 595, 623, 655, 707, 755, 773`).

### Step 2: Define real `ReadScopes` / `WriteScopes` (`app/bots/base.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Replace** the placeholder dataclasses with:
   ```python
   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class ReadScopes:
       topics: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"own"})
       allow_cross_topic_peek: bool = False
       allow_cross_topic_status_injection: bool = False

   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class WriteScopes:
       topics: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"own"})
       require_reason_for_cross_topic: bool = False
   ```
2. **Add** `cross_topic_policy: str | None = None` to `BotSpec` (values: `None | "peek" | "write"`). Bump `bot_spec_version` to `"1.1.0"`.
3. **Add** `BotSpec.tool_allowlist: frozenset[str] | None = None` (used by Step 12 for per-bot tool gating). `None` means "no extra filter".

### Step 3: Mediator BotSpec gets real scopes (`app/bots/mediator.py`, `app/bots/registry.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Set** the mediator instantiation (`app/bots/mediator.py:99`):
   ```python
   MEDIATOR_BOT = MediatorBotSpec(
       bot_id="mediator",
       prompt_renderer=render_system_prompt,
       step_instructions=MEDIATOR_STEP_INSTRUCTIONS,
       cross_topic_policy="peek",
       read_scopes=ReadScopes(
           topics=frozenset({"own"}),
           allow_cross_topic_peek=True,
           allow_cross_topic_status_injection=True,
       ),
       write_scopes=WriteScopes(
           topics=frozenset({"relationship"}),
           require_reason_for_cross_topic=True,
       ),
   )
   ```
2. **Mirror** these values in `populate_mediator_spec_from_db` (`app/bots/registry.py:70-71`). Don't pass `tool_allowlist` — mediator default `None` is correct.

### Step 4: Plumb scopes onto every production TurnContext build site (`app/services/agentic.py`, `app/services/recovery.py`, `app/services/scheduled_jobs.py`, `app/services/checkins.py`, `app/services/inbound.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Find** every `TurnContext(...)` in `app/` (`grep -n "TurnContext(" app/`).
2. **For each production site** that already passes `bot_spec=spec`: also pass `read_scopes=spec.read_scopes`, `write_scopes=spec.write_scopes`, `cross_topic_policy=spec.cross_topic_policy`. Adding these as keyword args is a one-line touch per site.
3. **Do not** touch test fixtures here (they intentionally pass `None` for scopes — the gate helper in Step 5 is None-permissive).

### Step 5: Scope-guard helper + gate at function entry (`app/services/tools/scope_guard.py`, `app/services/tools/read_tools.py`, `app/services/tools/write_tools.py`, `tool_schemas.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `app/services/tools/scope_guard.py`:
   ```python
   from app.services.turn_context import TurnContext

   def check_read_scope(ctx: TurnContext, requested_scope: str) -> str | None:
       """Return None if allowed, else an error message.

       Resolution: requested_scope='own' resolves to ctx.primary_topic_slug.
       The bot's read_scopes.topics may contain the literal token 'own' (a
       bot-relative wildcard meaning 'whatever this bot's primary topic is'),
       the literal 'all' (cross-topic), or explicit slugs. If ctx.read_scopes
       is None, gate is permissive (legacy fixtures).
       """
       if ctx.read_scopes is None:
           return None
       topics = ctx.read_scopes.topics
       if "all" in topics:
           return None
       if requested_scope == "all":
           return "scope_denied: bot lacks 'all' read scope"
       # requested_scope == "own" or an explicit slug
       target_slug = ctx.primary_topic_slug if requested_scope == "own" else requested_scope
       if "own" in topics and target_slug == ctx.primary_topic_slug:
           return None
       if target_slug in topics:
           return None
       return f"scope_denied: topic '{target_slug}' not in read scope"

   def check_write_scope(ctx: TurnContext) -> str | None:
       if ctx.write_scopes is None:
           return None
       topics = ctx.write_scopes.topics
       if "all" in topics:
           return None
       slug = ctx.primary_topic_slug
       if "own" in topics:  # write 'own' shorthand mirrors read
           return None
       if slug in topics:
           return None
       return f"write_scope_denied: topic '{slug}' not in write scope"

   ARTIFACT_READ_TOOLS = frozenset({
       "get_memories", "get_observations", "get_distillations",
       "get_oob", "summarize_oob_topics", "list_themes", "get_theme",
       "list_watch_items", "list_bridge_candidates", "get_self_model",
   })
   ARTIFACT_WRITE_TOOLS = frozenset({
       "add_memory", "update_memory", "supersede_memory",
       "create_theme", "update_theme",
       "add_watch_item", "update_watch_item", "address_watch_item",
       "log_observation", "update_observation",
       "add_distillation", "update_distillation", "revise_distillation",
       "add_oob", "update_oob", "lift_oob",
       "create_bridge_candidate", "update_bridge_candidate", "send_bridge_candidate",
       "update_user_style_notes", "update_cross_thread_sharing_default",
       "set_topic_status",
   })
   ```
2. **Add** `scope: Literal["own", "all"] = "own"` to the 10 artifact-reader input models in `tool_schemas.py`. Do **not** add anything to `TOOL_DESCRIPTIONS`.
3. **At the function entry** of each of the 10 artifact-reader functions in `read_tools.py`, call:
   ```python
   err = check_read_scope(ctx, args.scope)
   if err:
       return <OutputModel>(is_error=True, error=err, ...)  # populate empty lists
   ```
   The exact empty-output shape is per-tool (e.g. `GetMemoriesOutput(is_error=True, error=err, memories=[])`).
4. **At the function entry** of each of the ~21 artifact-writer functions in `write_tools.py`, call:
   ```python
   err = check_write_scope(ctx)
   if err:
       raise ToolCallRejected({"error": err})
   ```
5. **Do not** add a gate to: `search_messages`, `search_emojis`, `recent_activity`, `get_bot_actions`, `check_oob`, `send_message_part`, `consult_perspective`, `list_scheduled_tasks`, `send_message_part`, `react_to_message`, `edit_outbound_message`, `delete_outbound_message`, `escalate_to_partner`, `log_feedback`, `explain_media_item`, `schedule_checkin`, `cancel_scheduled_checkin`, `schedule_task`, `update_scheduled_task`, `cancel_scheduled_task`. Locked decision #7 + FLAG-S4-002.

### Step 6: Production-builder lint (`tests/test_s4_scope_lint.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Test 1**: walk `TOOL_DISPATCH` (excluding `update_turn_plan`), assert each callable's first positional arg is named `ctx` and the body source contains `ctx.`.
2. **Test 2**: import the production builders (`agentic.py`, `recovery.py`, `scheduled_jobs.py`, `checkins.py`, `inbound.py`) and grep their source for any `TurnContext(` constructor that does **not** include `read_scopes=` and `write_scopes=` on the same call. Fail if found. (Test-only constructors are exempt; this scans `app/` only — per FLAG-S4-005, legacy test fixtures are deliberately permissive.)
3. **Test 3**: instantiate a fresh mediator-shaped `TurnContext` (with scopes populated) and verify `check_read_scope(ctx, "own")` returns `None` (sanity).

## Phase 3: `topic_status` write/read surface

### Step 10: `set_topic_status` schema (`tool_schemas.py`, `app/services/tools/registry.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** to `tool_schemas.py`:
   ```python
   class SetTopicStatusInput(BaseModel):
       scope: Literal["user", "dyad"]
       user_id: UUID | None = None
       headline: str = Field(..., max_length=80)
       body: str = Field("", max_length=300)

   class SetTopicStatusOutput(BaseModel):
       is_error: bool = False
       error: str | None = None
       status_id: UUID | None = None
       headline: str | None = None
       body: str | None = None
       updated_at: str | None = None
   ```
2. **Register** in `TOOL_REGISTRY`, `TOOL_DISPATCH`, and `WRITE_PHASE_TOOLS`. **Do not** add to `RECORD_WRITE_TOOLS` directly — it derives from `WRITE_PHASE_TOOLS - SCHEDULE_TOOLS` (`registry.py:223`), so adding to `WRITE_PHASE_TOOLS` is sufficient. (correctness-5 fix.)
3. **Add** description to `TOOL_DESCRIPTIONS`: "Update or replace the current status for this topic. Headline ≤ 80 chars, body ≤ 300 chars. Use at most once per turn during the `record` step when status has materially changed."

### Step 11: Write impl (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Implement** `set_topic_status(ctx, args)`:
   - First-line gate: `check_write_scope(ctx)` (artifact writer per Step 5).
   - Validate XOR: `scope=="user"` ⇒ `args.user_id is not None`; `scope=="dyad"` ⇒ `ctx.dyad_id is not None`. Reject with `ToolCallRejected` otherwise.
   - Upsert SQL **must include `last_updated_by_bot_id`** (NOT NULL per `migrations/0022_topic_status_user_bot_state.sql:18`). correctness-3 fix:
     ```python
     # user-scoped:
     row = await ctx.pool.fetchrow(
         """
         INSERT INTO topic_status
             (topic_id, user_id, headline, body, last_updated_by_bot_id, last_updated_at)
         VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, now())
         ON CONFLICT (topic_id, user_id) WHERE user_id IS NOT NULL DO UPDATE
         SET headline = EXCLUDED.headline,
             body = EXCLUDED.body,
             last_updated_by_bot_id = EXCLUDED.last_updated_by_bot_id,
             last_updated_at = now()
         RETURNING id, headline, body, last_updated_at
         """,
         ctx.primary_topic_id, args.user_id, args.headline, args.body, ctx.bot_id,
     )
     # dyad-scoped: same shape, with (topic_id, dyad_id) and ON CONFLICT (topic_id, dyad_id) WHERE dyad_id IS NOT NULL.
     ```
   - Confirm the partial unique indexes (`migrations/0022:24-30`) match the ON CONFLICT predicates exactly. If asyncpg rejects partial-index ON CONFLICT predicates without the WHERE clause, fall back to a SELECT-then-INSERT-OR-UPDATE pattern (named in the impl notes).
   - Pydantic already enforces lengths; the runtime guard is redundant but harmless — keep the Pydantic-only check (drop the duplicate runtime guard from the prior plan revision).
   - Return populated `SetTopicStatusOutput` from `row`.
2. **Log** via `_log_tool_call` (`write_tools.py:127`).

### Step 12: Mediator records status (`app/bots/mediator.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Append** to `MEDIATOR_RECORD_INSTRUCTION` (`app/bots/mediator.py:74`): "If this turn has materially shifted the relationship's current state, call `set_topic_status` at most once with a short headline (≤80 chars) and optional body (≤300 chars). Otherwise omit it."
2. Coach `tool_allowlist` (Step 18) will omit `set_topic_status`. Per-bot enforcement leverages `BotSpec.tool_allowlist` added in Step 2.3 — gate it inside `_step_allowed(ctx)` (`registry.py:270`): `if ctx.bot_spec is not None and ctx.bot_spec.tool_allowlist is not None: allowed &= ctx.bot_spec.tool_allowlist`.

### Step 13: Tests (`tests/test_set_topic_status.py`, `tests/test_scope_negative.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Test set_topic_status**: happy paths for user and dyad scopes (with `ctx.bot_id="mediator"`); upserts on second call; rejection on body > 300 / headline > 80; XOR violation rejection.
2. **Negative-permission test (the §16.5 stop-condition fixture)**: build a `BotSpec` with `read_scopes.topics=frozenset({"career"})`, `write_scopes.topics=frozenset({"career"})`, attach to a `TurnContext` with `primary_topic_slug="relationship"`. Call `get_memories`, `list_themes`, `get_oob`, and `add_memory` through `call_tool(...)`. Expected results:
   - Reads: `is_error=True`, `error` starts with `"scope_denied"`.
   - Writes: `is_error=True`, `error` starts with `"write_scope_denied"`.
   This is the test the brief names as a stop condition if it *passes* (returns artifacts) — the revised Step 5 gate (which checks `primary_topic_slug ∈ topics` with `"own"` as wildcard) now correctly fires.
3. **Positive-permission regression**: mediator-shaped ctx (`read_scopes.topics={"own"}`, `primary_topic_slug="relationship"`) calls the same tools and gets `is_error=False`.
4. **Legacy-fixture regression test**: build a `TurnContext` with `read_scopes=None, write_scopes=None`, call `get_memories` and `add_memory`. Both must succeed (gate is None-permissive). Protects against FLAG-S4-005.

## Phase 4: Hot context — status block + cross-topic peek code paths

### Step 14: Status block render (own topic) (`app/services/hot_context.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** field to `HotContext` (`hot_context.py:24`): `topic_status: dict[str, Any] | None = None`.
2. **Fetch** with priority: dyad row first when `ctx.dyad_id` is set (`WHERE topic_id=$1 AND dyad_id=$2`), else user row (`WHERE topic_id=$1 AND user_id=$2`). Use the existing pool.
3. **Render** under `## Topic status` in `render_hot_context`: include `headline`, `body` (if non-empty), and `last_updated_at`. With no row → emit nothing (no empty header).

### Step 15: Cross-topic status injection + peek (`app/services/hot_context.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add helper** `fetch_cross_topic_status(pool, *, dyad_id, user_id, exclude_topic_id, cap=5)`: SELECT from `topic_status` joined to `topics` for slug, WHERE matching dyad or user AND `topic_id <> exclude_topic_id`, ORDER BY `last_updated_at DESC` LIMIT cap. With one topic in prod, returns `[]`.
2. **Add helper** `peek_other_topics(pool, *, dyad_id, user_id, exclude_topic_id, since: datetime, cap=5)`: thin summary keyed on `topic_status.last_updated_at >= since`. The 14d window (locked decision A) is computed as `now - timedelta(days=14)` in Python and passed as `$N`.
3. **Gate calls** behind `ctx.bot_spec.read_scopes.allow_cross_topic_status_injection` and `ctx.bot_spec.read_scopes.allow_cross_topic_peek` respectively. Mediator allows both; with empty result → no header rendered.
4. **Tests** in `tests/test_hot_context_cross_topic.py`: stubbed pool returning rows → blocks render; empty → no headers.

## Phase 6: Solo "coach" bot — staging-only pre-flight

### Step 17: Staging seed migration (`migrations/0031_coach_staging_seed.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Write** the migration with **all DML inside a single DO block** so the `IF current_database() …` guard actually protects everything (FLAG-S4-004 / correctness-4 fix):
   ```sql
   BEGIN;

   DO $$
   BEGIN
       IF current_database() NOT LIKE '%staging%' THEN
           RAISE NOTICE 'coach seed: skipping on non-staging database %', current_database();
           RETURN;
       END IF;

       INSERT INTO channels (id, display_name, provider, ...) VALUES (...)
           ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING;
       INSERT INTO bots (id, display_name, ...) VALUES ('coach', 'Coach', ...)
           ON CONFLICT (id) DO NOTHING;
       INSERT INTO topics (slug, display_name, ...) VALUES ('career', 'Career', ...)
           ON CONFLICT (slug) DO NOTHING;
       INSERT INTO bot_bindings (bot_id, topic_slug, channel_id, ...) VALUES ('coach', 'career', ...)
           ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING;
   END $$;

   COMMIT;
   ```
2. **Header comment**: "Applies only when `current_database() LIKE '%staging%'`. S5 owns the prod promotion. All DML lives inside the DO block so the RETURN guard actually protects it."
3. **Confirm** the staging DB name actually contains `staging` (open verification — encode as Assumption #5 and a `should` success criterion; if the name doesn't match, the migration is a no-op everywhere, which is safe).

2. **Phase 1** (Steps 1-6) — authorization scaffolding, including the None-permissive gate helper and production-builder lint.

Major plan changes: (1) Rewrote the read-scope gate to actually fire for default `scope=\"own\"` by resolving against `ctx.primary_topic_slug` and treating `\"own\"` in `read_scopes.topics` as a self-referential wildcard \u2014 the negative-permission stop-condition test now correctly trips. (2) Restricted the write-scope gate to an explicit `ARTIFACT_WRITE_TOOLS` set; transport, scheduling, `log_feedback`, etc., are explicitly excluded. (3) Made the gate helper None-permissive so the ~15 legacy test fixtures without `read_scopes`/`write_scopes` keep passing; added a positive legacy-fixture regression test (Step 13.4). (4) Added a new production-builder lint (Step 6.2) that scans `app/` constructors to enforce scope plumbing \u2014 test fixtures in `tests/` are deliberately exempt. (5) `set_topic_status` upsert now includes `last_updated_by_bot_id=ctx.bot_id` (NOT NULL in migrations/0022). (6) Removed misleading \"register in RECORD_WRITE_TOOLS\" wording \u2014 `RECORD_WRITE_TOOLS` is derived, registering in `WRITE_PHASE_TOOLS` is sufficient. (7) Rewrote the staging migration to place **all DML inside a single DO block** so the `current_database() LIKE '%staging%'` RETURN-guard actually protects every statement. (8) Resolved Q1 (stash the dirty `tool_schemas.py` first, treat it as in-scope on a clean base) and Q4 (numbered file `0031_coach_staging_seed.sql` with in-SQL guard, no staging subfolder) and folded both into the plan. (9) Acknowledged the locked-decision-6 input_schema exposure trade-off explicitly in Overview. (10) Added a `BotSpec.tool_allowlist` plumb-in for the coach to omit `set_topic_status`. Removed `app/services/cross_thread_privacy.py` from Step 15's target list (not actually needed). Success criteria adjusted to cover the gate semantics, the None-permissive path, the bot_id-NOT-NULL fix, and the all-DML-inside-DO migration shape.",

The Step 13.2 negative test with coach `topics={\"career\"}` and `primary_topic_slug=\"relationship\"` now correctly trips the deny branch (since `\"own\"` is not in topics, and `\"relationship\"` is not in topics, and `\"all\"` is not in topics)."

The undocumented-first rollout strategy mitigates.\n- Status injection token budget. Enforce headline \u2264 80, body \u2264 300 in `set_topic_status`.\n- `consult_perspective` is the easiest leak path \u2014 the enumeration test is MANDATORY.\n- Solo pre-flight runs in parallel; if it blocks (e.g. Discord bot account approval delays), defer those items to S5 with explicit TODOs but DON'T expand S5 by more than ~3 days.\n\n**Deliberately not in this sprint.**\n\nMulti-topic writes via `topic_slugs`. Solo bot prompt or hot context code (lands in S5). Account-linking workflow. PROD promotion of the solo bot (S5).\n\n## S1-S3 lessons the executor must honor\n\n1. **CLI scripts**: invoke with `--help` to confirm parseable.\n2. **SQL fragments in Python strings**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` or sanity-check types (no `max(uuid)` etc.).\n3. **asyncpg pool against `DATABASE_URL`**: pass `statement_cache_size=0` (Supabase port 6543 is transaction-mode).\n4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs** \u2014 use `hashlib.sha1`.\n5. **Schema filters in validation queries** use `'mediator'` not `'public'`.\n6. **For psql migrations against Supabase**: use port 5432 session-mode for DDL.\n7. **Don't introduce scope creep**: no unrelated features or unused fields in shared classes.\n8. **NO defensive fallbacks**: per S3 lesson, don't add `topic_id IS NULL` or similar permissive filters.\n9. **For verify-human-heavy plans**: keep criterion count manageable (~10-15); each criterion should be cheaply auto-verifiable from artifacts.\n10. **S3 cutover is committed**: every artifact read goes through `topic_filter.join_artifact_topics()`. New code follows this pattern, don't reintroduce direct reads.\n11. **Pytest baseline**: 534 pass / 0 fail / 3 skip on s3-join-cutover HEAD. S4 must maintain or improve.\n\n## Stop conditions\n\n- Any test that was passing on `s3-join-cutover` HEAD (commit 52d6dab) starts failing \u2014 STOP, report.\n- `consult_perspective` enumeration test fails to enumerate any field \u2014 STOP, fix (this is a real correctness gap).\n- Tool-schema changes break mediator behavior in eval (if eval suite available) \u2014 STOP, audit.\n- Solo bot infra accidentally touches prod (any non-staging DB write) \u2014 STOP, revert.\n- Negative-permission test (career-scoped bot trying to read relationship artifacts) PASSES (it should FAIL \u2014 that's the point) \u2014 STOP, fix authorization layer.",

        "resolution": "Addressed all 12 significant flags. (2) Restricted the write-scope gate to an explicit `ARTIFACT_WRITE_TOOLS` set; transport, scheduling, `log_feedback`, etc., are explicitly excluded. (3) Made the gate helper None-permissive so the ~15 legacy test fixtures without `read_scopes`/`write_scopes` keep passing; added a positive legacy-fixture regression test (Step 13.4). (4) Added a new production-builder lint (Step 6.2) that scans `app/` constructors to enforce scope plumbing \u2014 test fixtures in `tests/` are deliberately exempt. (5) `set_topic_status` upsert now includes `last_updated_by_bot_id=ctx.bot_id` (NOT NULL in migrations/0022). (6) Removed misleading \"register in RECORD_WRITE_TOOLS\" wording \u2014 `RECORD_WRITE_TOOLS` is derived, registering in `WRITE_PHASE_TOOLS` is sufficient. (7) Rewrote the staging migration to place **all DML inside a single DO block** so the `current_database() LIKE '%staging%'` RETURN-guard actually protects every statement.         Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-019",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-026",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-031",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-034",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 4.5's `_row_matches_topic` helper falls back to global behavior when (table, id) is not in self.artifact_topics \u2014 explicitly for backward compatibility. this is sensible but creates a subtle hazard: a test that forgets to call link_topic for a new artifact sees global decay behavior with no warning, which could mask the very scoping bug the new test in step 12 is meant to catch. the decay scope test does call link_topic for both topics, so it's safe; future authors may not. worth recording but not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-3-join-cutover"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-036",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: flag-017 (decay handler backward-compat fallback) is downgraded to accepted maintainability debt with an inline warning comment per step 4.5. the warning text explicitly tells future authors to call link_topic. acceptable, but a noisy assertion (e.g. log a warning when topic_id present in sql but row has no link) would catch silent regressions more loudly. not blocking.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-3-join-cutover"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-001",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",

      {
        "id": "DEBT-001",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-002",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
